# Roadmap

The live board of every development item the plan, its ADRs and its experiments name: what is done and what closed it, what is under way, what is open, what is blocked and by what, and what a recorded decision has deferred. Each row is a title and a pointer; what the item is, and why, lives at the pointer. The history of estimates against actuals stays in the ledger ([10 §10.8](plan/10-roadmap-risks.md#108-calibrating-the-estimates)); this page is the current state.

**Keeping it current.** A change that finishes, starts or blocks a roadmap item updates its row here in the same commit, and a new piece of work gets a row before it starts ([AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#rules-that-are-checked), "Documentation moves with the code"). `tools/docs-check.ps1` holds the page to its shape: ids are unique, every id a "Depends on" cell names is a row, a `done` row names what closed it, a `blocked` row says by what, every row has a Where link that resolves, and State, Effort and Reasoning use the words below.

## How to read it

| Column | Values |
|---|---|
| `#` | A stable id: the area's letter and a number. An id is never renumbered or reused, so a dependency can name it; a new item takes its area's next free number wherever its row goes. |
| Item | The title. |
| State | `done` (with the ADR, commit or dated status note that closed it), `in progress`, `open`, `blocked` (by what), `deferred` (a decision is recorded not to build it now). |
| Depends on | The ids it needs first. Empty means it can start now. |
| Effort | `S`: hours, one agent's stage. `M`: a day, one agent. `L`: several agents or batches. |
| Reasoning | `routine`: the pattern exists in the tree and the spec is clear, a smaller model's task. `design`: choices to make, trade-offs to weigh. `research`: unknowns, and an experiment decides. |
| Where | The plan section, ADR, experiment or page to read first. |

**Blocked and open.** A row is `blocked` only by something outside ordinary work: the owner's review or decision, hardware, a runner, a session on the owner's machine. A row that waits on other rows is `open`, and its "Depends on" says on what. The work that can start today is therefore every `open` row whose dependencies are all `done`.

**Done is a claim with a source.** A `done` row names the ADR, the commit or the dated status note that closed it. Where only part of a plan item is built, the built part is a `done` row and the named remainder an `open` one. A proposed ADR whose decision is built shows twice: the built item, `done`, and its acceptance, `blocked` on the owner's review.

**Areas**, in roughly the order the plan builds things: [R, the renderer core and ray-traced lighting](#r--the-renderer-core-and-ray-traced-lighting); [W, the world, its streaming and the ground](#w--the-world-its-streaming-and-the-ground); [D, the data model and documents](#d--the-data-model-and-documents); [S, simulation and physics](#s--simulation-and-physics); [C, characters and tissue](#c--characters-and-tissue); [G, content and generation](#g--content-and-generation); [T, tooling and CI](#t--tooling-and-ci); [A, the agent protocol and tools](#a--the-agent-protocol-and-tools); [P, the reference games](#p--the-reference-games); [F, found along the way](#f--found-along-the-way), the open defects.

Compiled on 2026-10-07 from the plan's thirteen chapters and their dated status notes and addenda, the §10.8 ledger, the [ADR index](adr/README.md), the [experiment index](experiments/README.md), the subsystem pages' "Not yet" lists, and the git log since 2026-09-05.

The first lever, a light too faint to test (Ward 1991, `renderer.shadow.light_tolerance`), returned 12.4–15.4 ms of it (forest 33.0 → 20.6 ms, GPU frame 52.9 → 41.6) for one code in 0.01% of the pixels. **Stage 2 (2026-11-01)**: the owner's v25 walk (markers 1 → 2) flown as a path at both widths with every stage and every sun-ray rule in one flight (`renderer.shadow.sweep`) and the sun ray counted per pixel (the stop word's counting bit): at 11520 the resolve is 22.5 of a 42.9 ms GPU frame, the sun's ray 14.6 of it and its masks 12.2; a ray samples 1.6 masks on average (99th percentile 12), so the cost is each decision's dependent chain and the warp's longest ray. The mesh rasterizer saves the trace's 2 ms and the frame keeps it in its pool pass. The mask at the ray's footprint was built, measured slower (+1.7 to +10.5 ms) with 4–26% of the pixels moved, and removed; no proxy threshold buys time without moving the shadow (0.5 already moves 4.4%). Opacity micromaps: the cluster build takes them (`opacityMicromapArray` per cluster), built once per mesh — the lever, the chain's to build. The offscreen replay does not reach the owner's 38 ms resolve (22.5 at 11520 over the same 1.47 million clusters); the game module's run with the split would say why. The policy (`RtCapacity`, R107: grow to demand plus 25 % headroom, shrink after 240 frames below half) is to (a) grow to the larger of that and the capacity the chain held in the last minute, so a view that returns regrows in one step, (b) shrink by wall time, never below a floor the recent peak sets, and never from a frame a shrink would make drop, (c) make the new buffers before the wait and swap them at a frame boundary, keeping the old ones for the frames in flight, so a resize costs no `wait_idle`; the flight replayed, a handful of resizes and no frame short | done (2026-11-02; 423b2697 the peak, the wall-time window with its frames, the floor at half, and the resize swapped in beside the old set when the cache's references hold and the memory budget has room: on the walk's first 4,200 frames replayed three times, 51 capacity resizes become 9 and the 32 frames short of their demand under the budget none; the resize's cost was the wait and the free, never the CLAS set; the deferred free still lands on the frame's thread — [the experiment](experiments/rt-capacity-2026-11-02.md)) | R107, R114 | M | design | [renderer](subsystems/renderer.md#the-ray-tracing-chains-memory) |

the read at half rate in the side columns is a structure change, requested. **Stage 3 (batch 57) held at the gate, 2026-11-09**: `b57-R69-3` (5decb5ab, on eeb8077c) built the turn's measurement from the v36 cave mouth, a read cascade over five levels, probes round the eye, the read's integers without a 64-bit division, a wave-shared read and two tail knobs, all off by default because none met the bound; stage 2's read does from frame 55). Batch 58's first R69 item, from that branch rebased: the defaults byte-identical to stage 2b and invariant first (the read's integers and the shared corner hashes are the suspects), then the cascade's invariance (its coarse levels' cells against the 64 m world cell), then the tail. **Stage 4 (batch 58, 2026-11-09), in progress**: batch 58 took the owner's v37 turn instead of stage 3's branch (left unmerged, its section carried to [the page](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-3-the-fill-on-a-turn-and-the-reads-cost) as a negative result): a caster volume round the eye (`renderer.gi.shell_m`, `eye_shell.h`) and samples over the sphere from the eye (`renderer.gi.shell_rays`), both off by default — on the v37 `south-lake` turn they took four fifths of the bias away but not the fill (1.68 → 1.76 against a no-turn floor of 0.43: the variance of few-sample cells), and cost 0.8 ms at 3840×2160 at 48 m; in the box from inside the first frame after a turn is 0.018 of the term from converged against main's 0.071 ([stage 4](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-4-the-light-round-the-eye)); the cache's counts now fold on pipelined frames, and they showed the 32 MiB cache full on v37, so `renderer.gi.cache_mib` defaults to 128, which alone takes a fifth of the turn's fill away (1.68 → 1.30). Done: the pages split, the ice's grounding on a page of its own; what the ice carries laid as a ridge across the valley at each snout with its river's gate left open (a ridge across the line sent the snout to the region's edge), capped at the published 60 m and the glacier's thickness, and a lodged sheet of a twohundredth of the ice's thickness that rides with the ice through its wear, so the basins are no longer buried (the firn valley holds its water in rock); `ice.stands`; the fields `ice_max`, `moraine`, `moraine_crest`, `lakebed` and `rock_floor` in `engine.landform.fields/4`, and an ice map from the command; beside its line a glacier wears nothing outside the published parabola through its swath, so the trough test reads b = 1.9 inside 1.5 to 2.5 where it read 4.9, and a tributary's step stands where its bed meets the trunk's section (22 m) and not where the swath's margin sat on a lattice point. Not done: **the two-range sketch at its ice age as sketched still ends with no lake** and with 20 points of moraine (a basin fills as the ice draws back, a sill and a ridge are worn away within steps, and its ice is 96 m at the thickest); lateral moraines; a tributary's own ice in its hanging step, and a test valley with a thin tributary; cirques; a steady glacier) | W126 | L | research | [landform](subsystems/landform.md), [grounding](experiments/landform-ice.md) |

| S93 | The sea to the horizon, and an ocean surface grounded in wave spectra: a `WaterBody` may be `open` (its polygon its shore, the same water past it to the horizon wherever the ground is below the level, drawn by the resolve as an analytic plane, the waves continuous across the outline); then Gerstner waves sampled from JONSWAP with Donelan–Banner spreading, FFT cascades, underwater | in progress (2026-10-31: the grounding and the design, [ocean-surface](experiments/ocean-surface.md); bd1dcbc5, stage 1 — an `open` body's sea drawn past its outline to the horizon by the resolve as a plane at its level; and stage 1b — an open body's waves sixteen Gerstner waves from JONSWAP at the wind and its fetch with Donelan–Banner spreading, held to a double-precision reference; remaining: FFT cascades, underwater, a mask for hollows below the level, the plane in the ids and depth channels) | S63, S86 | L | research | [ocean-surface](experiments/ocean-surface.md) |
| S94 | The water's edge where it meets the ground (the owner, 2026-11-02): today the shore is a band `shore_m` wide inside the outline where the colour fades from the bottom alone to the reflecting surface ([water](subsystems/water.md), "The shore") and the terrain's wetness within a body's `wet_m` is one darkening; neither moves, and the waterline is the outline's, not the waves'. A lake, a river or a sea has no tension boundary: its edge is the wave-displaced waterline against the ground's height, so the line advances and retreats with every wave, and the swash band it leaves is wet ground that dries with the time since it was last covered (W72's wet darkening under an age, or a decay by height above the mean line scaled by the wave's amplitude); the surface fades to the bottom over the last wavelength, not over `shore_m`; only a still small body (a puddle, a trough) keeps a meniscus. Research first, cited on its page (swash and run-up on a beach: Stockdon et al. 2006 for the ocean; a lake's wind-wave run-up a fraction of its wave height); the moving line is the resolve's, the water's and the terrain material's one function, every rasterizer and the ray path alike. Begun 2026-11-08 (batch 55): the signed depth map (version 2), the wind's swash by Stockdon's run-up, a ballistic front in wave groups, the wet band dried by the clock in closed form, the foam riding the front, an open body's M2 tide ([water](subsystems/water.md#the-swash), grounding [shore](experiments/shore.md#the-swash); 13358247). Not built: the mesh flooded above the still level (the sheet over the bank is drawn on the ground's pixels), the wet ground's roughness (S99), a closed body's surface from the spectrum, a tide drawn with volume, a channel's swash. 2026-11-08 (S99): the swash's darkening, its sheet's and film's after-lighting sky reflection and its gloss moved into the ground's material (a saturation and a film handed in before the lighting, the film's sky reflection a lighting term), the foam kept; a bared pixel is the ground's own material, not a 0.35 sand. 2026-11-08 (batch 57): continues in S100 — the mesh lifted and grown over the bank, the swash's phase the wave train's | in progress | S86, S93, R99 | M | design | [water](subsystems/water.md) |
| S95 | Flowing water: rivers, streams and currents (the owner, 2026-11-02; G38 lays roads and rivers as spline networks, and no row moves the water): a river is a channel the ground provider carves along the terrain's drainage network (flow accumulation over the heightfield, D8 or D-infinity; stream order; the channel's width and depth from its discharge by hydraulic geometry, Leopold and Maddock 1953; its velocity by Manning's equation from slope, depth and roughness) and a `WaterBody` of a new kind whose surface carries a flow field — the waves and the normal advected along it by a two-phase flow map so the advection never stretches (Vlachos 2010), foam where the flow meets an obstacle or breaks over a step, the level following the channel's profile rather than one height — with S94's edge; research first, cited on its page, the picture before the sim's side (S63's buoyancy and swimming against a current) | open | S63, S94, G38 | L | research | [water](subsystems/water.md) |

Not in it: mist (R47's medium), plunge foam where a channel ends at its fall's foot, a jump or a bend in the surface's shape, wading in a channel, confluences. 2026-11-07: the owner found the flowing reaches tiled and not reading as water; the surface's look, the brink, the plunge stretch and the plunge into a pool are S98's | done (bcb8e927, 6bf20820, 059ddb0c) | S63, S93 | M | design | [water](subsystems/water.md) |

Built 2026-11-07 (30f4bfd3, 298b74be, 62d67f1e): the fill, the per-pixel waterline, the wet band and the foam line; not built: option (b), the wet band's roughness, a terrain mask region naming a body that fills | in progress | S63, S86, S93 | M | design | [water](subsystems/water.md) |

Not done: the banks' saturation baked per terrain tile at load (one texel read in place of the tree walk, the structure the stream's cost wants: about 1.3 ms at 1080p and 3.8 ms at 4K at v35's stream before d90f9780, the cut's paired numbers in [shore](experiments/shore.md#what-it-costs-and-what-it-looks-like) where they were taken) | S94, S98 | M | research | [terrain](subsystems/terrain.md#wet-ground), [shore](experiments/shore.md#wet-ground) |
| S100 | The wave as height, and the swash that rides it (the owner, 2026-11-08: the sea's swash "far too rapid and chaotic (and even forms loops sometimes instead of a steady wave), and there's no height change as the waves come in"; the lake's "hard cutoff"): a water body with a wind is a deformed instance whose pool stage (`k_deform_stage_water`) lifts its mesh by one height function — the body's waves offshore, near the shore the wind's peak wave shoaled by Green's law, broken at 0.78 d (McCowan) and slowed by the dispersion relation along an arrival field solved at load — its normal that function's gradient, a ringed body's mesh grown over the bank as a rim whose vertices are the swash's sheet or parked under the ground, and the swash's phase the train's arrival instead of a per-place drift. Stage A committed 2026-11-08 (batch 57: 7b8bbe27, cb28d403, 094a9f5d, rebased in batch 58 onto R141 as ea1d1aa1, 0392557f, 90951304; [water](subsystems/water.md#the-mesh), grounding [shore](experiments/shore.md#the-wave-as-height)) and **usable since batch 58** (2026-11-09): efc347bb writes the deform table's per-frame copies with the table — the forest's device loss was a streamed tree's record, made when only the part-pose table outgrew its room, in no copy (the same v37 run clean after it, `lifted_tail_tests.cpp`); 5b454967 gives a scene whose only moving thing is a windy body the deform pass; 3cab7b6d lets a sine with no length add nothing. On v37 (R141 against stage A, both msvc-release copied out of this checkout, four held views at 1920 × 1080 and 3840 × 2160, three repeats, others' CPU 21–34%, upper bounds) the pool pass costs +0.002 to +0.006 ms, the resolve −0.17 to +0.20 ms and the frame stays within ±0.2 ms. The 8 fps sequences (main against stage A at shore-open, lake-bank and eastern-coast; [water](subsystems/water.md#the-mesh)) show what stage B is for. **Stage B** (2026-11-10, batch 59; [shore](experiments/shore.md#stage-b-waves-that-read-as-water)): every body with a wind draws the fetch-limited spectrum — a closed body over its own reach along its wind (`gfx::water_fetch_field`), the fetch laws stopped at full development — each of the sixteen waves a narrow band (a carrier under a travelling envelope, so nothing lines up or repeats), with a lee where the wind has not yet blown far, and the slope a pixel cannot hold given to the lobe's roughness; the shallows' faceted crests counted (a bilinear field read turned 203 corners in 170 m of crest; the mesh was lifted by waves a third of a vertex long; an uncovered rim vertex was parked at once) and fixed by derivation (a C1 read of the field, rings graded from a quarter of the breaking bore's wavelength at the outline, the lift held against the mesh's own spacing, the sheet's surface carried on under the ground past the front); foam born where each wave of the group reaches 0.78 d and nowhere else, as a closed form of the clock with a 4 s e-folding that thins to a lace, a thin scalloped front line on the uprush, and none for a wave under 2 cm or one that surges (Iribarren past 3.3) — no stamp map: the train is periodic, so time alone gives it; the density a wave leaves and its life follow its height, the waterline is the front's per pixel, and the wavelets a pixel cannot hold come back at the pixel's own scale so far water is never a mirror under a wind (7d197531, cba54156; the resolve +0.11 ms at 1080p and +0.40 ms at 4K with a lake over half the frame, +0.05 and +0.23 ms for a sea, paired; the pictures and what is still wrong in them are on the shore page). Not done: the three defects the 4K frames show (a dark rim where bared water meets filmed sand, flat shallows inshore of the break, the depth map's contours in a lake's wet margin), a swell (`swell_height_m`, `swell_period_s`: the record has the word, nothing sets it), a rim and graded rings for a body that fills, foam that drifts with the water, a breaking crest's forward lean. POC migration: `foam_m`, `wave_amplitude_m`, `wave_length_m` and `chop` are no longer read for a body with a wind; a closed body's fetch is its own reach, so `fetch_km` only shortens it | in progress | S94, S93, S86 | M | design | [water](subsystems/water.md) |
